I'm actually not sure if this is related to the request data caching vs offloading you mentioned, so any advice would be appreciated. Our request body is very small FWIW, \u003c 10 lines of XML. If my issue is not related to the cache vs offloading, I think this reflects different behaviours of the two HTTP Conduits. \r\n\r\nI will give the information I can but need to redact/change some things.\r\n\r\n**Unit test mocks**:\r\n```\r\nprivate void mockRedirect(...) throws IOException {\r\n    wireMockServer.stubFor(\r\n            WireMock.post(urlPathEqualTo(\"/normal/test/path\"))\r\n                    .withBasicAuth(VALID_USERNAME, String.valueOf(VALID_PASSWORD))\r\n                    .withHeader(...)\r\n                    .withHeader(...)\r\n                    .withRequestBody(matchingXPath(...))\r\n                    .atPriority(1)\r\n                    .willReturn(\r\n                            aResponse()\r\n                                    .withStatus(302)\r\n                                    .withHeader(\"location\", wireMockServer.baseUrl() + \"/redirect-test\")));\r\n  }\r\nprivate void mockRedirectFinal(...) throws IOException {\r\n    wireMockServer.stubFor(\r\n            WireMock.post(urlPathEqualTo(\"/redirect-test\"))\r\n                    .withBasicAuth(VALID_USERNAME, String.valueOf(VALID_PASSWORD))\r\n                    .withHeader(...)\r\n                    .withHeader(...)\r\n                    .withRequestBody(matchingXPath(...))\r\n                    .willReturn(\r\n                            aResponse()\r\n                                    .withStatus(200)\r\n                                    .withHeader(\"content-type\", \"application/xml\")\r\n                                    .withBodyFile(\"sample-file.xml\")));\r\n  }\r\n```\r\n\r\n**Tests done**: \r\nquarkus 3.15, 3.16 and 3.17 with URLConnectionHTTPConduit and VertxHTTPConduit\r\n\r\n**Results**: \r\n3.15: \r\nI only tested the default URLConectionHTTPConduit here. Test passes as expected.\r\n\r\n3.16: \r\nThe default VertxHTTP fails with a 302 Error as expected. \r\nThe URLConnectionHTTP passes as expected.\r\n\r\n3.17:\r\nThe URLConnectionHTTP passes.\r\nThe VertxHTTP fails with a 404 Error. Stack trace:\r\n```\r\nCaused by: org.apache.cxf.transport.http.HTTPException: HTTP response '404: Not Found' when communicating with http://localhost:50575/redirect-test\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$ResponseHandler.doProcessResponseCode(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:1272)\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$ResponseHandler.handle(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:1176)\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$ResponseHandler.handle(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:1154)\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$RequestBodyHandler$Mode$Sync.awaitResponse(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:1044)\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$RequestBodyHandler.handle(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:564)\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$RequestBodyHandler.handle(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:425)\r\n\tat io.quarkiverse.cxf.vertx.http.client.VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit$RequestBodyOutputStream.close(VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.java:420)\r\n\tat org.apache.cxf.ext.logging.LoggingOutputStream.postClose(LoggingOutputStream.java:53)\r\n\tat org.apache.cxf.io.CachedOutputStream.close(CachedOutputStream.java:228)\r\n\tat org.apache.cxf.transport.AbstractConduit.close(AbstractConduit.java:56)\r\n\tat org.apache.cxf.transport.http.HTTPConduit.close(HTTPConduit.java:717)\r\n\tat org.apache.cxf.interceptor.MessageSenderInterceptor$MessageSenderEndingInterceptor.handleMessage(MessageSenderInterceptor.java:63)\r\n\t... 103 more\r\n```\r\n\r\nUpon further investigation, removal of  .withRequestBody(matchingXPath(...)) in the stub of RequestFinal() made the test pass. So it seems like it did redirect the request, but the body is not included in the redirected request? I tried to look through the source code but could not find the reason quickly. \r\n\r\nPS: One tiny bug I did notice is that in VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.redirectRetransmit, both the URLs in the debug log refer to the redirection URL (in the previous method the new URL is already added to the list, so list.size()-1 is the new URL, not the old one). \r\n\r\nPPS: If it's not too difficult to implement, being able to switch on logging the redirection responses and requests might also help with debugging. \r\n\r\nThanks a lot for your help again. <p dir="auto">I just did a test and unfortunately there still seems to be an issue at the unit test level. I'm actually not sure if this is related to the request data caching vs offloading you mentioned, so any advice would be appreciated. Our request body is very small FWIW, &lt; 10 lines of XML. If my issue is not related to the cache vs offloading, I think this reflects different behaviours of the two HTTP Conduits.</p>
<p dir="auto">I will give the information I can but need to redact/change some things.</p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Unit test mocks</strong>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="private void mockRedirect(...) throws IOException {

<p dir="auto"><strong>Tests done</strong>:<br>
quarkus 3.15, 3.16 and 3.17 with URLConnectionHTTPConduit and VertxHTTPConduit</p>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Results</strong>:<br>
3.15:<br>
I only tested the default URLConectionHTTPConduit here. Test passes as expected.</p>
<p dir="auto">3.16:<br>
The default VertxHTTP fails with a 302 Error as expected.<br>
The URLConnectionHTTP passes as expected.</p>
<p dir="auto">3.17:<br>
The URLConnectionHTTP passes.<br>
The VertxHTTP fails with a 404 Error. Stack trace:</p>

<p dir="auto">Upon further investigation, removal of  .withRequestBody(matchingXPath(...)) in the stub of RequestFinal() made the test pass. So it seems like it did redirect the request, but the body is not included in the redirected request? I tried to look through the source code but could not find the reason quickly.</p>
<p dir="auto">PS: One tiny bug I did notice is that in VertxHttpClientHTTPConduit.redirectRetransmit, both the URLs in the debug log refer to the redirection URL (in the previous method the new URL is already added to the list, so list.size()-1 is the new URL, not the old one).</p>
<p dir="auto">PPS: If it's not too difficult to implement, being able to switch on logging the redirection responses and requests might also help with debugging.</p>

data-turbolinks="false"><div class="markdown-body safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto">Thanks for the reproducer, <a class="user-mention notranslate" data-hovercard-type="user" data-hovercard-url="/users/dcheng1248/hovercard" data-octo-click="hovercard-link-click" data-octo-dimensions="link_type:self" href="https://github.com/dcheng1248">@dcheng1248</a>! The issue was that the body was cached twice if there was just a single buffer used to pass the original body. See the fix in <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" data-error-text="Failed to load title" data-id="2708289118" data-permission-text="Title is private" data-url="https://github.com/quarkiverse/quarkus-cxf/issues/1631" data-hovercard-type="pull_request" data-hovercard-url="/quarkiverse/quarkus-cxf/pull/1631/hovercard" href="https://github.com/quarkiverse/quarkus-cxf/pull/1631">#1631</a> . It is actually strange that our CXF test service happily ignored the second XML document in the request body. I had to adapt the test to run against a plain dummy vert.x endpoint to be able to reproduce it.</p></div></div><div role="toolbar" aria-label="Reactions" class="d-flex gap-1 <p dir="auto">Regarding your question, honestly I'm not 100% sure. In our case this is towards a system we don't manage - I wasn't even aware redirection was occurring until I got that 302 Error on quarkus 3.16. That said, I can think of two possible use cases in general.</p>
<ol dir="auto">
<li>Session management. It can be through cookies or just internal routing of the load balancer, but afaik a 302 to the same URL is possible in both cases.</li>
<li>As you said, resource management/ health check that results in load balancer redirection.</li>

safe-html-box__Uwc6Al Container__ZAxOBo"><p dir="auto">In our use case probably not. Auth retransmit deals with 401 or 407, while we get a redirection loop error (not a auth redirection loop) that stems from the same url according to the stack trace.</p>
<p dir="auto">Since the apache implementation also doesn't seems to deal with session cookie headers from 302 (from my quick scan of the source, please correct if I'm wrong), I doubt that's the issue. I would more guess in our case it's either an LB port/server routing through IPHashing etc. or a LB resource management that causes the redirection to the same URL.</p></div></div><div role="toolbar"
